| Input: wings\_enabled | null | {&quot;default&quot;: &quot;false&quot;, &quot;description&quot;: &quot;\[experimental\] Opt in to the mise-wings asset cache\\n(https://mise-wings.en.dev) for this action invocation.\\n\\nWhen \`true\`, the action exports \`MISE\_WINGS\_ENABLED=1\` so\\nthe installed mise binary routes tool-install URLs (npm\\ntarballs, GitHub release artifacts) through the per-org\\nwings cache subdomains.\\n\\nAuthentication is automatic via the runner&#x27;s GitHub OIDC\\nidentity — no \`mise wings login\` step, no long-lived\\nsecret to rotate. The workflow must declare\\n\`permissions: id-token: write\` so the OIDC token-issuer\\nenv vars are populated; without that, mise falls through\\nto direct-origin fetches transparently.\\n\\nDefault \`false\` is the conservative posture: a workflow\\nwith \`id-token: write\` (used for SLSA / AWS-OIDC /\\nSigstore / etc.) should not have its OIDC token sent to\\na third-party cache without explicit opt-in. Older mise\\nbinaries that don&#x27;t speak wings ignore the env var\\nentirely, so this is forward-compatible.\\n\\nRequires an active mise-wings subscription on the Clerk\\norg linked to the GitHub org running the workflow;\\nwithout one, the proxy 402s and mise leaves the cache\\noff without affecting the workflow&#x27;s success.\\n&quot;, &quot;required&quot;: false} |

| Input: safety-strategy | {&quot;default&quot;: &quot;drop-sudo&quot;, &quot;description&quot;: &quot;Specify one of the following options (on Windows, the only supported option is \`unsafe\`):\\n\\n\* \`drop-sudo\` (default, IRREVERSIBLE) Drop sudo privileges (if any) from\\n  the default user before running Codex, and run Codex as that user. This\\n  is only supported on Linux and macOS runners. This option is\\n  irreversible: if the default user has sudo privileges, they will be\\n  removed permanently for the duration of the job.\\n\* \`unprivileged-user\` Run Codex as the specified user specified by the\\n  \`codex-user\` option (the user must already exist). Note the caller is\\n  responsible for ensuring the specified user has the privileges it needs\\n  to perform the requested actions. For example, the copy of the repo\\n  created by \`actions/checkout\` is not world-readable by default.\\n\* \`read-only\` Run Codex in a sandbox that can read any file on disk,\\n  but cannot write to disk or access the network. Note Codex will still\\n  run as the default user for this Action, which likely has sudo\\n  privileges, so it could read \`openai-api-key\` from memory and reveal it\\n  by printing it to the output of the GitHub Action.\\n\* \`unsafe\` (NOT RECOMMENDED) Do not try to restrict Codex&#x27;s privileges at all.\\n  This is extremely dangerous, as the default user for this Action likely\\n  has sudo privileges, which means it can read secrets stored in memory\\n  (such as the value of \`openai-api-key\`) and print them to the output\\n  of the GitHub Action or exfiltrate them in other ways.\\n&quot;, &quot;required&quot;: false} | {&quot;default&quot;: &quot;drop-sudo&quot;, &quot;description&quot;: &quot;Specify one of the following options (on Windows, the only supported option is \`unsafe\`):\\n\\n\* \`drop-sudo\` (default, IRREVERSIBLE) Drop sudo privileges (if any) from\\n  the default user before running Codex, and run Codex as that user. This\\n  is only supported on Linux and macOS runners. This option is\\n  irreversible: account and host changes can outlive a job on reused\\n  self-hosted runners. On Linux, access to\\n  existing root-owned service sockets under \`/run\` that the runner can\\n  write, including Docker, is also removed. On Linux, Codex starts with\\n  \`no\_new\_privs\`, no supplementary groups, and empty host-namespace\\n  capability sets. Descendants inherit \`no\_new\_privs\`; capabilities in\\n  new user namespaces do not grant host privileges. Bubblewrap-backed\\n  modes require unprivileged user namespaces; setuid-only bubblewrap\\n  installations are not supported.\\n\* \`unprivileged-user\` Run Codex as the specified user specified by the\\n  \`codex-user\` option (the user must already exist). Note the caller is\\n  responsible for ensuring the specified user has the privileges it needs\\n  to perform the requested actions. For example, the copy of the repo\\n  created by \`actions/checkout\` is not world-readable by default.\\n\* \`read-only\` Run Codex in a sandbox that can read any file on disk,\\n  but cannot write to disk or access the network. Note Codex will still\\n  run as the default user for this Action, which likely has sudo\\n  privileges, so it could read \`openai-api-key\` from memory and reveal it\\n  by printing it to the output of the GitHub Action.\\n\* \`unsafe\` (NOT RECOMMENDED) Do not try to restrict Codex&#x27;s privileges at all.\\n  This is extremely dangerous, as the default user for this Action likely\\n  has sudo privileges, which means it can read secrets stored in memory\\n  (such as the value of \`openai-api-key\`) and print them to the output\\n  of the GitHub Action or exfiltrate them in other ways.\\n&quot;, &quot;required&quot;: false} |
| Input: sandbox | {&quot;default&quot;: &quot;workspace-write&quot;, &quot;description&quot;: &quot;Sandbox mode for Codex. | Changed | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Input: hide-no-changes-pr-comment | null | {&quot;default&quot;: &quot;false&quot;, &quot;description&quot;: &quot;Whether to suppress the \\&quot;Release Plan\\&quot; pull request comment when the pull request has nothing to report. A pull request has \\&quot;nothing to report\\&quot; when it changes no Terraform modules, has no pending tag/release cleanup (when delete-legacy-tags is enabled), and the wiki check did not fail. By default this is false, preserving the existing behavior where a \\&quot;Release Plan\\&quot; comment is always posted on every open pull request run.\\nWhen set to true and a pull request has nothing to report:\\n  - If no Release Plan comment exists yet, none is created (and therefore no email notification\\n    is sent).\\n  - If a Release Plan comment already exists (e.g. an earlier push had changes that were later\\n    removed), it is updated in place and minimized/collapsed. Because this is an edit to an\\n    existing comment, GitHub does not send a new email notification.\\n\\nWhen the pull request does have changes, the Release Plan comment is posted exactly as before (recreated at the bottom of the timeline to signal a fresh run). This setting only affects the open-pull-request Release Plan comment; the post-merge release comment is unaffected.\\n&quot;, &quot;required&quot;: true} |
| Observed stability | unverified | observed |

| Selected tag | v2.1.0 | v2.2.0 |
